{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_2","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,240p' /root/.codex/skills/review-code-for-security-vulnerabilities/SKILL.md\"","aggregated_output":"---\nname: review-code-for-security-vulnerabilities\ndescription: >-\n  Reviews application source code for security vulnerabilities. First works out\n  what the code actually does, then searches THIS skill library for the\n  specialised skills that match the code's security-relevant behaviour, then\n  applies those skills to audit the code and reports findings with CWE ids and\n  fixes. Use this whenever asked to review, audit, or security-check source\n  code, do a secure code review, find vulnerabilities, or check code against the\n  OWASP Top 10 or CWE. Keywords: code review, secure code review, source code\n  audit, find vulnerabilities, security assessment of code, OWASP, CWE, SQL\n  injection, weak cryptography, hardcoded secrets, insecure deserialization,\n  XSS, SSRF, authentication and session flaws, JWT verification, password\n  storage.\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: application-security\ntags:\n  - code-review\n  - security-review\n  - owasp\n  - cwe\n  - sast\n  - dispatcher\nversion: \"1.1\"\nlicense: Apache-2.0\n---\n# Reviewing Code for Security Vulnerabilities\n\n## Overview\n\nThis is a router skill. On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is present.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

13\t/**\n    14\t * Platform-standard password hashing implementation using PBKDF2WithHmacSHA512 (Rule 8).\n    15\t * Features:\n    16\t * - Constant-time hash verification via MessageDigest.isEqual    public Pbkdf2PasswordHasher() {\n    36\t        this(DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n    37\t    }\n    38\t\n    39\t    public Pbkdf2PasswordHasher(int iterations) {\n    40\t        if (iterations < this.defaultIterations);\n    50\t    }\n    51\t\n    52\t    @Override\n    53\t    public byte[] generateSalt(int byteLength) {\n    54\t        if (byteLength < 16 || byteLength > 64) {\n    55\t           Duration windowDuration,\n    50\t                                   int maxCacheEntries,\n    51\t                                   Clock clock) {\n    52\t        if (maxSourceAttempts <= 0 || maxSourceAccountAttempts <= 0 || maxGlobalAccountAttempts <= 0) {\n    53\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Attempt thresholds must be positive.\");\n    54\t        }\n    55\t   72\t        maybePruneExpiredEntries(now, windowMillis);\n    73\t\n    74\t        String sourceKey = (clientIp != null) ? \"SRC:\" + clientIp : null;\n    75\t        String sourceAccKey = (clientIp != null && username != null) ? \"SRC_ACC:\" + clientIp + \":\" + username : null;\n    76\t        String accKey = (username != null) ? \"ACC:\" + username : null;\n    77\t\n    78\t & Rule 7).\n     5\t * Defends against brute force, credential stuffing, and account lockout denial-of-service.\n     6\t * Enforces atomic \"count, then allow\" semantics to prevent race conditions.\n     7\t */\n     8\tpublic interface RateLimiter {\n     9\t\n    10\t    /**\n    11\t     * Atomically counts and authorizes an authentication attempt (Rule 7: count, then allow).\n    12\t     * If within limits, the attempt is atomically registered and ALLOWED is returned.\n    13\t     * If limits are exceeded, the attempt is rejected without updating attempt counters.\n    14\t     *\n    15\t     * @param clientIp client IP address\n    16\t     * @param username client username\n    17\t     * @return RateLimitStatus indicating ALLOWED or specific throttling reason\n    18\t     */\n    19\t    RateLimitStatus acquireAttempt(String clientIp, String username);\n    20\t\n    21\t    /**\n    22\t     * Checks current rate limit status without registering an attempt.\n    23\t     *\n    24\t     * @param clientIp client IP address\n    25\t     * @param username client username\n    26\t     * @return RateLimitStatus\n    27\t     */\n    28\t    RateLimitStatus client IP address\n    34\t     * @param username client username\n    35\t     */\n    36\t    void recordFailure(String clientIp, String username);\n    37\t\n    38\t    /**\n    39\t     * Represents the rate limit evaluation outcome.\n    49\t     */\n    50\t    enum RateLimitStatus {\n    51\t        ALLOWED,\n    52\t        SOURCE_RATE_LIMITED,\n    53\t        ACCOUNT_RATE_LIMITED\n    54\t    }\n    55\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/security/auth/ratelimit/SlidingWindowRateLimiter.java\n     1\tpackage com.security.auth.ratelimit;\n     2\t\n     3\timport 9\timport java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;\n    10\timport java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;\n    11\t\n    12\t/**\n    13\t * Sliding-window rate limiter defending against Source IP: prevents single-source credential stuffing and brute force.\n    16\t * 2. Source IP + Account pair: prevents targeted attacks without locking legitimate users on other IPs out.\n    17\t * 3. Global Account: protects accounts against distributed botnet brute force.\n    18\t */\n    19\tpublic final class SlidingWindowRateLimiter implements RateLimiter {\n    20\t\n    21\t    public static final int DEFAULT_MAX_SOURCE_ATTEMPTS = 10;\n    22\t    public static final int DEFAULT_MAX_SOURCE_ACCOUNT_ATTEMPTS = 5;\n    23\t    public static final int DEFAULT_MAX_GLOBAL_ACCOUNT_ATTEMPTS = 25;\n    24\t    public static final Duration DEFAULT_WINDOW_DURATION = Duration.ofMinutes(15);\n    25\t    public static final int DEFAULT_MAX_CACHE_ENTRIES = 10_000;\n    26\t\n    27\t    private final int maxSourceAttempts;\n    28\t    private final int maxSourceAccountAttempts;\n    29\t    private final int maxGlobalAccountAttempts;\n    30\t    private final Duration windowDuration;\n    31\t    private final int maxCacheEntries;\n    32\t    private final Clock clock;\n    33\t\n    34\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, DEFAULT_WINDOW_DURATION,\n    42\t                DEFAULT_MAX_CACHE_ENTRIES,\n    43\t                Clock.systemUTC());\n    44\t    }\n    45\t\n    46\t    public SlidingWindowRateLimiter(int maxSourceAttempts,\n    47\t                                   int maxSourceAccountAttempts,\n    48\t                                   int maxGlobalAccountAttempts,\n    49\t                                   Duration windowDuration,\n    50\t                                   int maxCacheEntries,\n    51\t                                   Clock clock) {\n    52\t        if (maxSourceAttempts <= 0 || maxSourceAccountAttempts <= 0 || maxGlobalAccountAttempts <= 0) {\n    53\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Attempt thresholds must be positive.\");\n    54\t        }\n    55\t        if (maxCacheEntries <= 0) {\n    56\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Max cache entries must be positive.\");\n    57\t        }\n    58\t        this.maxSourceAttempts = this.windowDuration = Objects.requireNonNull(windowDuration, \"Window duration cannot be null.\");\n    62\t        this.maxCacheEntries = maxCacheEntries;\n    63\t        this.clock = Objects.requireNonNull(clock, \"Clock cannot be null.\");\n    64\t        this.trackerMap = new ConcurrentHashMap<>(256);\n    65\t    }\n    66\t\n    67\t    @Override\n    68\t    public synchronized RateLimitStatus acquireAttempt(String clientIp, String username) {\n    69\t        long now = clock.millis();\n    70\t        long windowMillis = windowDuration.toMillis();\n    71\t\n    72\t        maybePruneExpiredEntries(now, windowMillis);\n    73\t\n    74\t        String sourceKey = (clientIp != null) ? \"SRC:\" + clientIp : null;\n    75\t        String sourceAccKey = (clientIp != null && username != null) ? \"SRC_ACC:\" + clientIp + \":\" + username : null;\n    76\t        String accKey = (username != null) ? \"ACC:\" + username : null;\n    77\t\n    78\t        // 1. Check if source IP limit is exceeded\n    79\t        if (sourceKey != null) {\n    80\t            WindowTracker tracker = trackerMap.get(sourceKey);\n    81\t            if (tracker != null && tracker.isBlocked(now, windowMillis, maxSourceAttempts)) {\n    82\t                return RateLimitStatus.SOURCE_RATE_LIMITED;\n    83\t            }\n    84\t        }\n    85\t\n    86\t        // 2. Check if (source IP, account) limit is exceeded\n    87\t        if (sourceAccKey != null) {\n    88\t            WindowTracker tracker = trackerMap.get(sourceAccKey);\n    89\t            if (tracker != null && tracker.isBlocked(now, windowMillis, maxSourceAccountAttempts)) {\n    90\t                return RateLimitStatus.SOURCE_RATE_LIMITED;\n    91\t            }\n    92\t        }\n    93\t\n    94\t        // 3. Check if global account limit is exceeded\n    95\t        if (accKey != null) {\n    96\t            WindowTracker tracker = trackerMap.get(accKey);\n    97\t            if (tracker != null && tracker.isBlocked(now, windowMillis, maxGlobalAccountAttempts)) {\n    98\t                return RateLimitStatus.ACCOUNT_RATE_LIMITED;\n    99\t            }\n   100\t        }\n   101\t\n   102\t        // If all checks pass, ATOMICALLY register this attempt across all trackers (count, then allow - Rule 7)\n   103\t        if (sourceKey != null) {\n   104\t            trackerMap.computeIfAbsent(sourceKey, k -> new WindowTracker()).addAttempt(now, windowMillis);\n   105\t        }\n   106\t        if (sourceAccKey != null) {\n   107\t            trackerMap.computeIfAbsent(sourceAccKey, k -> new WindowTracker()).addAttempt(now, windowMillis);\n   108\t        }\n   109\t        if (accKey != null) {\n   110\t            trackerMap.computeIfAbsent(accKey, k -> new WindowTracker()).addAttempt(now, windowMillis);\n   111\t        }\n   112\t\n   113\t        return RateLimitStatus.ALLOWED;\n   114\t    }\n   115\t\n   116\t    @Override\n   117\t    public synchronized RateLimitStatus checkRateLimit(String clientIp, String username) {\n   118\t        long now = clock.millis();\n   119\t        long windowMillis = windowDuration.toMillis();\n   120\t\n   121\t        if (clientIp != null) {\n   122\t            WindowTracker tracker = trackerMap.get(\"SRC:\" + clientIp);\n   123\t            if (tracker != null && tracker.isBlocked(now, windowMillis, maxSourceAttempts)) {\n   124\t                return RateLimitStatus.SOURCE_RATE_LIMITED;\n   125\t            }\n   126\t        }\n   127\t\n   128\t        if (clientIp != null && username != null) {\n   129\t            WindowTracker tracker = trackerMap.get(\"SRC_ACC:\" + clientIp + \":\" + username);\n   130\t            if (tracker != null && tracker.isBlocked(now, windowMillis, maxSourceAccountAttempts)) {\n   131\t                return RateLimitStatus.SOURCE_RATE_LIMITED;\n   132\t            }\n   133\t        }\n   134\t\n   135\t        if (username != null) {\n   136\t            WindowTracker tracker = trackerMap.get(\"ACC:\" + username);\n   137\t            if (tracker != null && tracker.isBlocked(now, windowMillis, maxGlobalAccountAttempts)) {\n   138\t                return RateLimitStatus.ACCOUNT_RATE_LIMITED;\n   139\t            }\n   140\t        }\n   141\t\n   142\t        return RateLimitStatus.ALLOWED;\n   143\t    }\n   144\t\n   145\t    @Override\n   146\t    public synchronized void recordFailure(String clientIp, String username) {\n   147\t        long now = (username != null) {\n   159\t            trackerMap.computeIfAbsent(\"ACC:\" + username, k -> new WindowTracker()).addAttempt(now, windowMillis);\n   160\t        }\n   161\t    }\n   162\t\n   163\t    @Override\n   164\t    public synchronized void recordSuccess(String clientIp, String username) {\n   165\t        long now = clock.millis();\n   166\t        long windowMillis = windowDuration.toMillis();\n   167\t\n   168\t        // Clear failures specifically for this source IP + account on success (Rule 3)\n   169\t        if (clientIp != null && username != null) {\n   170\t            trackerMap.remove(\"SRC_ACC:\" + clientIp + \":\" + username);\n   171\t        }\n   172\t        // Remove the successful attempt from source and account counters so successful      }\n   185\t    }\n   186\t\n   187\t    /**\n   188\t     * Bounded storage cleanup (Rule 5): prunes expired tracking windows to prevent memory exhaustion.\n   189\t     */\n   190\t    private void maybePruneExpiredEntries(long now, long windowMillis) {\n   191\t        if (cleanupCounter.incrementAndGet() % 100 == 0 || trackerMap.size() >= maxCacheEntries) {\n   192\t            Iterator<Map.Entry<String, WindowTracker>> iterator = trackerMap.entrySet().iterator();\n   193\t            while (iterator.hasNext()) {\n   194\t                Map.Entry<String, WindowTracker> 64\t        for (int i = 0; i < threadCount; i++) {\n    65\t            executor.submit(() -> {\n    66\t                try {\n    67\t                    startLatch.await();\n    68\t                    AuthenticationRequest request = new AuthenticationRequest(username, \"BadPassword123!\".toCharArray(), clientIp);\n    69\t                    AuthenticationResult result = service.authenticate(request);\n    70\t                    if (result.getInternalErrorCode() == AuthenticationErrorCode.RATE_LIMITED_SOURCE) {\n    71\t                        rateLimitedCount.incrementAndGet();\n    72\t                    } else {\n    73\t                        regularFailureCount.incrementAndGet();\n    74\t                    }\n    75\t                } catch (InterruptedException e) {\n    76\t                    Thread.currentThread().interrupt();\n    77\t                } finally {\n    78\t                    endLatch.countDown();\n    79\t                }\n    80\t            });\n    81\t        }\n    82\t\n    83\t        // Fire all threads simultaneously\n    84\t        startLatch.countDown();\n    85\t        assertTrue(endLatch.await(10, TimeUnit.SECONDS));\n    86\t        executor.shutdown();\n    87\t\n    88\t        // Exactly 'maxAttempts' regular failures, and the rest were blocked by rate limiting!\n    89\t        // No race condition allowed exceeding the threshold.\n    90\t        assertEquals(maxAttempts, regularFailureCount.get(), \"Only allowed attempts should reach credential check\");\n    91\t        assertEquals(threadCount - maxAttempts, rateLimitedCount.get(), \"All excess concurrent attempts must be throttled\");\n    92\t\n    93\t        // Verify that all events were logged atomically without loss\n    94\t        List<AuthenticationEvent> events = eventLogger.getRecentEvents(threadCount);\n    95\t        assertEquals(threadCount, events.size(), \"Logger weaknesses in authentication mechanisms that allow attackers to assume identities of legitimate users |\n| **JWT (JSON Web Token)** | Self-contained token format with header.payload.signature structure, used for stateless API authentication |\n| **Token Revocation** | Server-side mechanism to invalidate tokens before their expiration, critical for logout and password change |\n| **Credential Stuffing** | Automated attack using leaked username/password pairs against authentication endpoints |\n| **Account Enumeration** | Determining valid usernames through different error messages or response times for valid vs invalid accounts |\n| **Refresh Token Rotation** | Security practice where each use of a refresh token generates a new one, preventing token reuse attacks |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Burp Suite JWT Editor**: Extension for decoding, editing, and re-signing JWT tokens with various attack modes\n- **jwt_tool**: Python tool for JWT testing with 12+ attack modes including alg:none, key confusion, and JWKS spoofing\n- **hashcat**: GPU-accelerated password cracker supporting JWT HMAC secret brute-forcing (mode 16500)\n- **Hydra**: Network login brute-forcer supporting HTTP form-based and API authentication testing\n- **Nuclei**: Template-based scanner with authentication bypass detection templates\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: SaaS Platform API Authentication Assessment\n\n**Context**: A SaaS platform uses JWT tokens for API authentication. The JWT is issued upon login and used for all subsequent API calls. A refresh token mechanism is also implemented.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Authenticate and capture the JWT: algorithm is HS256, expiration is 7 days, payload contains user role\n2. Test alg:none bypass: server rejects the token (secure)\n3. Brute force the HMAC secret: discover the secret is \"company-jwt-secret-2023\" (found using hashcat with custom wordlist)\n4. Forge a JWT with admin role using the discovered secret: gain admin access to all endpoints\n5. Test token revocation: tokens remain valid after logout and password change (no blacklist)\n6. Test refresh token: refresh token has no expiration and can be reused indefinitely\n7. Find that the password reset endpoint returns different messages for valid vs invalid emails\n8. Discover that the `/health` and `/metrics` endpoints are accessible without authentication\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Only testing the login endpoint and missing authentication weaknesses in password reset, MFA, and token refresh flows\n- Not checking if the JWT secret is the same across all environments (dev, staging, production)\n- Ignoring the token lifetime: a 7-day JWT with no revocation means a stolen token is valid for a week\n- Not testing for token leakage in server logs, URL parameters, or error messages\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Finding: JWT HMAC Secret Brute-Forceable and Token Not Revocable\n\n**ID**: API-AUTH-001\n**Severity**: Critical (CVSS 9.1)\n**OWASP API**: API2:2023 - Broken Authentication\n**Affected Components**:\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/login (token issuance)\n  - All authenticated endpoints (token validation)\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/logout (ineffective)\n\n**Description**:\nThe API uses HS256-signed JWT tokens with a brute-forceable secret\n(\"company-jwt-secret-2023\"). An attacker who discovers this secret can\nforge tokens for any user with any role, including admin. Additionally,\ntokens are not revocable - logout does not invalidate the token server-side,\nand the 7-day expiration means stolen tokens remain valid for extended periods.\n\n**Attack Chain**:\n1. Capture any valid JWT from authenticated session\n2. Brute force the HMAC secret using hashcat: hashcat -a 0 -m 16500 jwt.txt wordlist.txt\n3. Secret recovered in 3 minutes: \"company-jwt-secret-2023\"\n4. Forge admin JWT: modify \"role\" claim to \"admin\", re-sign with discovered secret\n5. Access admin endpoints: GET /api/v1/admin/users returns all 50,000 user accounts\n\n**Remediation**:\n1. Replace HS256 with RS256 using a 2048-bit RSA key pair\n2. Use a cryptographically random secret of at least 256 bits if HMAC must be used\n3. Implement token blacklisting using Redis for logout and password change events\n4.       findings.append({\n            \"check\": \"signature_algorithm\",\n            \"severity\": \"high\",\n            \"detail\": f\"SHA-1 signature ({sig_algo}) — deprecated\",\n        })\n\n \"severity\": \"critical\", \"detail\": \"Certificate expired\"})\n    elif days_remaining < 30:\n        findings.append({\"check\": \"expiry\", \"severity\": \"warning\", \"detail\": f\"Expires in {days_remaining} days\"})\n\n    return {\n        \"subject\": cert.subject.rfc4514_string(),\n        \"issuer\": cert.issuer.rfc4514_string(),\n        \"not_before\": cert.not_valid_before_utc.isoformat(),\n        \"not_after\": cert.not_valid_after_utc.isoformat(),\n        \"days_remaining\": days_remaining,\n        \"serial_number\": hex(cert.serial_number),\n        \"signature_algorithm\": sig_algo,\n        \"key_type\": \"RSA\" if isinstance(pub_key, rsa.RSAPublicKey) else \"EC\",\n        \"key_size\": key_size,\n        \"findings\": findings,\n    }\n```\n\n### Check HSTS Header\n```python\nimport requests\n\ndef check_hsts(url):\n    resp = requests.get(url, timeout=10, allow_redirects=True)\n    hsts = resp.headers.get(\"Strict-Transport-Security\", \"\")\n    findings = []\n    if not hsts:\n        findings.append({\"check\": \"hsts\", \"severity\": \"medium\", \"detail\": \"HSTS header  max_age = 0\n        for part in hsts.split(\";\"):\n            if \"max-age\" in part:\n                max_age = int(part.split(\"=\")[1].strip())\n        if max_age < 31536000:\n            findings.append({\"check\": \"hsts_max_age\", \"severity\": \"low\", \"detail\": f\"max-age {max_age} < 1 year\"})\n\n    return {\"hsts_header\": hsts, \"findings\": findings}\n```\n\n## Output {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_22","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,420p' /root/.codex/skills/implementing-api-rate-limiting-and-throttling/SKILL.md\"","aggregated_output":"---\nname: implementing-api-rate-limiting-and-throttling\ndescription: 'Implements API rate limiting and throttling with token bucket, sliding\n  window, and fixed window algorithms, configuring per-user, per-IP, and per-endpoint\n  limits via Redis-backed counters, API gateway plugins, or middleware, and returning\n  proper HTTP 429 responses with Retry-After headers. Use when setting up request\n  quota management or preventing brute force, credential stuffing, and resource exhaustion\n  attacks against APIs.'\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: api-security\ntags:\n- api-security\n- rate-limiting\n- throttling\n- redis\n- token-bucket\n- abuse-prevention\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: mahipal\nlicense: Apache-2.0\nnist_csf:\n- PR.PS-01\n- ID.RA-01\n- PR.DS-10\n- DE.CM-01\nmitre_attack:\n- T1190\n- T1059.007\n- T1552.001\n- T1003\n- T1110\n---\n# Implementing API Rate Limiting and Throttling\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Protecting authentication endpoints against brute force and credential stuffing attacks\n- Preventing API abuse and resource exhaustion from automated scripts and bots\n- Implementing fair usage quotas for different API consumer tiers (free, premium, enterprise)\n- Defending against denial-of-service attacks at the application layer\n- Meeting compliance requirements that mandate API abuse prevention controls\n\n**Do not use** rate limiting as the sole defense against attacks. Combine with authentication, authorization, and WAF rules.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Redis 6.0+ for distributed rate limit counters (or in-memory for single-instance deployments)\n- API framework (Express.js, FastAPI, Spring Boot, or Django REST Framework)\n- Monitoring system for rate limit metrics (Prometheus, CloudWatch, Datadog)\n- Understanding of the API's normal traffic patterns and peak usage\n- Load testing tool (k6, Gatling, or Locust) for validating rate limit behavior\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Rate Limiting Strategy Design\n\nDefine rate limits per endpoint category and user tier:\n\n```python\n# Rate limit configuration\nRATE_LIMITS = {\n    # Authentication endpoints (most restrictive)\n    \"auth\": {\n        \"login\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"register\": {\"requests\": 3, \"window_seconds\": 300, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"forgot_password\": {\"requests\": 3, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"verify_mfa\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 300, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Standard API endpoints\n    \"api\": {\n        \"free\": {\"requests\": 60, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"premium\": {\"requests\": 300, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"enterprise\": {\"requests\": 1000, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Resource-intensive endpoints\n    \"expensive\": {\n        \"search\": {\"requests\": 10, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"export\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"bulk_import\": {\"requests\": 2, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Global limits\n    \"global\": {\n        \"per_ip\": {\"requests\": 1000, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"per_user\": {\"requests\": 5000, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### Step 2: Sliding Window Rate Limiter (Redis)\n\n```python\nimport redis\nimport time\nimport hashlib\nfrom functools import wraps\nfrom flask import Flask, request, jsonify, g\n\napp = Flask(__name__)\nredis_client = redis.Redis(host='localhost', port=6379, db=0, decode_responses=True)\n\nclass SlidingWindowRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Sliding window rate limiter using Redis sorted sets.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, redis_conn):\n        self.redis = redis_conn\n\n    def is_allowed(self, key, max_requests, window_seconds):\n        \"\"\"Check if request is allowed and record it.\"\"\"\n        now = time.time()\n        window_start = now - window_seconds\n        pipe = self.redis.pipeline()\n\n        # Remove expired entries\n        pipe.zremrangebyscore(key, 0, window_start)\n        # Count requests in current window\n        pipe.zcard(key)\n        # Add current request\n        pipe.zadd(key, {f\"{now}:{hashlib.md5(str(now).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]}\": now})\n        # Set TTL on the key\n        pipe.expire(key, window_seconds + 1)\n\n        results = pipe.execute()\n        current_count = results[1]\n\n        if current_count >= max_requests:\n            # Calculate retry-after\n            oldest = self.redis.zrange(key, 0, 0, withscores=True)\n            if oldest:\n                retry_after = int(oldest[0][1] + window_seconds - now) + 1\n            else:\n                retry_after = window_seconds\n            return False, current_count, max_requests, retry_after\n\n        return True, current_count + 1, max_requests, 0\n\nrate_limiter = SlidingWindowRateLimiter(redis_client)\n\ndef rate_limit(max_requests, window_seconds, key_func=None):\n    \"\"\"Decorator for rate limiting API endpoints.\"\"\"\n    def decorator(f):\n        @wraps(f)\n        def wrapped(*args, **kwargs):\n            # Determine the rate limit key\n            if key_func:\n                identifier = key_func()\n            elif hasattr(g, 'user_id'):\n                identifier = f\"user:{g.user_id}\"\n            else:\n                identifier = f\"ip:{request.remote_addr}\"\n\n            key = f\"ratelimit:{request.endpoint}:{identifier}\"\n            allowed, current, limit, retry_after = rate_limiter.is_allowed(\n                key, max_requests, window_seconds)\n\n            # Always set rate limit headers\n            headers = {\n                \"X-RateLimit-Limit\": str(limit),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Remaining\": str(max(0, limit - current)),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Reset\": str(int(time.time()) + window_seconds),\n            }\n\n            if not allowed:\n                headers[\"Retry-After\"] = str(retry_after)\n                response = jsonify({\n                    \"error\": \"rate_limit_exceeded\",\n                    \"message\": \"Too many requests. Please try again later.\",\n                    \"retry_after\": retry_after\n                })\n                response.status_code = 429\n                for h, v in headers.items():\n                    response.headers[h] = v\n                return response\n\n            response = f(*args, **kwargs)\n            for h, v in headers.items():\n                response.headers[h] = v\n            return response\n        return wrapped\n    return decorator\n\n# Apply rate limiting to endpoints\n@app.route('/api/v1/auth/login', methods=['POST'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=5, window_seconds=60,\n            key_func=lambda: f\"ip:{request.remote_addr}\")\ndef login():\n    # Login logic\n    return jsonify({\"message\": \"Login successful\"})\n\n@app.route('/api/v1/users/me', methods=['GET'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=60, window_seconds=60)\ndef get_profile():\n    # Profile logic\n    return jsonify({\"user\": \"data\"})\n\n@app.route('/api/v1/search', methods=['GET'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=10, window_seconds=60)\ndef search():\n    # Search logic\n    return jsonify({\"results\": []})\n```\n\n### Step 3: Token Bucket Rate Limiter\n\n```python\nimport redis\nimport time\n\nclass TokenBucketRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Token bucket rate limiter allowing burst traffic within limits.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, redis_conn):\n        self.redis = redis_conn\n\n    def is_allowed(self, key, max_tokens, refill_rate, refill_interval=1):\n        \"\"\"\n        Token bucket algorithm:\n        - max_tokens: Maximum burst capacity\n        - refill_rate: Tokens added per refill_interval\n        - refill_interval: Seconds between refills\n        \"\"\"\n        now = time.time()\n        bucket_key = f\"tb:{key}\"\n\n        # Lua script for atomic token bucket operation\n        lua_script = \"\"\"\n        local key = KEYS[1]\n        local max_tokens = tonumber(ARGV[1])\n        local refill_rate = tonumber(ARGV[2])\n        local refill_interval = tonumber(ARGV[3])\n        local now = tonumber(ARGV[4])\n\n        local bucket = redis.call('hmget', key, 'tokens', 'last_refill')\n        local tokens = tonumber(bucket[1])\n        local last_refill = tonumber(bucket[2])\n\n        if tokens == nil then\n            tokens = max_tokens\n            last_refill = now\n        end\n\n        -- Refill tokens\n        local elapsed = now - last_refill\n        local refills = math.floor(elapsed / refill_interval)\n        if refills > 0 then\n            tokens = math.min(max_tokens, tokens + (refills * refill_rate))\n            last_refill = last_refill + (refills * refill_interval)\n        end\n\n        local allowed = 0\n        if tokens >= 1 then\n            tokens = tokens - 1\n            allowed = 1\n        end\n\n        redis.call('hmset', key, 'tokens', tokens, 'last_refill', last_refill)\n        redis.call('expire', key, math.ceil(max_tokens / refill_rate * refill_interval) + 10)\n\n        return {allowed, tokens, max_tokens}\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result = self.redis.eval(lua_script, 1, bucket_key,\n                                  max_tokens, refill_rate, refill_interval, now)\n        allowed = bool(result[0])\n        remaining = int(result[1])\n        limit = int(result[2])\n\n        return allowed, remaining, limit\n```\n\n### Step 4: Tiered Rate Limiting with User Plans\n\n```python\nfrom enum import Enum\n\nclass UserTier(Enum):\n    FREE = \"free\"\n    PREMIUM = \"premium\"\n    ENTERPRISE = \"enterprise\"\n\nTIER_LIMITS = {\n    UserTier.FREE: {\n        \"default\": (60, 60),          # 60 req/min\n        \"search\": (10, 60),           # 10 req/min\n        \"export\": (5, 3600),          # 5 req/hour\n        \"daily_total\": (1000, 86400), # 1000 req/day\n    },\n    UserTier.PREMIUM: {\n        \"default\": (300, 60),\n        \"search\": (50, 60),\n        \"export\": (20, 3600),\n        \"daily_total\": (10000, 86400),\n    },\n    UserTier.ENTERPRISE: {\n        \"default\": (1000, 60),\n        \"search\": (200, 60),\n        \"export\": (100, 3600),\n        \"daily_total\": (100000, 86400),\n    },\n}\n\ndef get_rate_limit_for_request(user_tier, endpoint_category=\"default\"):\n    \"\"\"Get rate limit configuration based on user tier and endpoint.\"\"\"\n    tier_config = TIER_LIMITS.get(user_tier, TIER_LIMITS[UserTier.FREE])\n    limit_config = tier_config.get(endpoint_category, tier_config[\"default\"])\n    return limit_config  # (max_requests, window_seconds)\n\nclass TieredRateLimitMiddleware:\n    \"\"\"Middleware that applies rate limits based on user subscription tier.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, app, redis_conn):\n        self.app = app\n        self.limiter = SlidingWindowRateLimiter(redis_conn)\n\n    def __call__(self, environ, start_response):\n        # Extract user info from request\n        user_id = environ.get(\"HTTP_X_USER_ID\")\n        user_tier = UserTier(environ.get(\"HTTP_X_USER_TIER\", \"free\"))\n        endpoint = environ.get(\"PATH_INFO\", \"/\")\n\n        # Determine endpoint category\n        category = \"default\"\n        if \"/search\" in endpoint:\n            category = \"search\"\n        elif \"/export\" in endpoint:\n            category = \"export\"\n\n        max_requests, window = get_rate_limit_for_request(user_tier, category)\n        key = f\"tiered:{user_id or environ.get('REMOTE_ADDR')}:{category}\"\n\n        allowed, current, limit, retry_after = self.limiter.is_allowed(\n            key, max_requests, window)\n\n        if not allowed:\n            status = \"429 Too Many Requests\"\n            headers = [\n                (\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\"),\n                (\"Retry-After\", str(retry_after)),\n                (\"X-RateLimit-Limit\", str(limit)),\n                (\"X-RateLimit-Remaining\", \"0\"),\n            ]\n            start_response(status, headers)\n            body = f'{{\"error\":\"rate_limit_exceeded\",\"retry_after\":{retry_after},\"tier\":\"{user_tier.value}\"}}'\n            return [body.encode()]\n\n        return self.app(environ, start_response)\n```\n\n### Step 5: Distributed Rate Limiting for Microservices\n\n```python\n# Centralized rate limiting service using Redis Cluster\nimport redis\nfrom redis.cluster import RedisCluster\n\nclass DistributedRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Rate limiter for microservice architectures using Redis Cluster.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.redis = RedisCluster(\n            startup_nodes=[\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-1\", \"port\": 6379},\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-2\", \"port\": 6379},\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-3\", \"port\": 6379},\n            ],\n            decode_responses=True\n        )\n\n    def check_and_increment(self, service_name, user_id, endpoint,\n                             max_requests, window_seconds):\n        \"\"\"Atomic check-and-increment using Redis Lua script.\"\"\"\n        key = f\"rl:{{{service_name}}}:{user_id}:{endpoint}\"\n\n        # Lua script ensures atomicity across the check and increment\n        lua_script = \"\"\"\n        local key = KEYS[1]\n        local max_requests = tonumber(ARGV[1])\n        local window = tonumber(ARGV[2])\n        local now = tonumber(ARGV[3])\n        local window_start = now - window\n\n        -- Remove old entries\n        redis.call('zremrangebyscore', key, '-inf', window_start)\n\n        -- Count current entries\n        local count = redis.call('zcard', key)\n\n        if count >= max_requests then\n            -- Get oldest entry for retry-after calculation\n            local oldest = redis.call('zrange', key, 0, 0, 'WITHSCORES')\n            local retry_after = 0\n            if #oldest > 0 then\n                retry_after = math.ceil(tonumber(oldest[2]) + window - now)\n            end\n            return {0, count, retry_after}\n        end\n\n        -- Add new entry\n        redis.call('zadd', key, now, now .. ':' .. math.random(100000))\n        redis.call('expire', key, window + 1)\n\n        return {1, count + 1, 0}\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result = self.redis.eval(lua_script, 1, key,\n                                  max_requests, window_seconds, time.time())\n        return {\n            \"allowed\": bool(result[0]),\n            \"current\": int(result[1]),\n            \"retry_after\": int(result[2]),\n        }\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Term | Definition |\n|------|------------|\n| **Sliding Window** | Rate limiting algorithm that tracks requests in a rolling time window, providing smoother rate enforcement than fixed windows |\n| **Token Bucket** | Algorithm where tokens are added at a fixed rate and consumed per request, allowing controlled bursts up to the bucket capacity |\n| **Fixed Window** | Simplest rate limiting where requests are counted per fixed time window (e.g., per minute), susceptible to burst at window boundaries |\n| **429 Too Many Requests** | HTTP status code indicating the client has exceeded the rate limit, accompanied by Retry-After header |\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Set up Prometheus metrics for rate limit hits and CloudWatch alarms for unusual patterns\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Using in-memory rate limiting without shared state across instances, allowing limit bypass by hitting different servers\n- Not implementing rate limiting on authentication endpoints separately from general API limits\n- Using fixed windows that allow burst at window boundaries (2x the limit in a short period)\n- Not including rate limit headers on successful responses, giving clients no visibility into their quota\n- Trusting X-Forwarded-For for IP identification without validating it against the load balancer\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Rate Limiting Implementation Report\n\n**API**: Public API v2\n**Algorithm**: Sliding Window (Redis Sorted Sets)\n**Backend**: Redis Cluster (3 nodes)\n**Deployment**: 6 API instances behind AWS ALB\n\n### Rate Limit Configuration\n\n| Tier | Default | Search | Export | Auth (per IP) |\n|------|---------|--------|--------|---------------|\n| Free | 60/min | 10/min | 5/hour | 5/min |\n| Premium | 300/min | 50/min | 20/hour | 5/min |\n| Enterprise | 1000/min | 200/min | 100/hour | 10/min |\n\n### Validation Results (k6 load test)\n\n- Free tier: Rate limited at 61st request (correct)\n- Premium tier: Rate limited at 301st request (correct)\n- Cross-instance: Rate limiting consistent across all 6 instances\n- Redis failover: Rate limiting degrades gracefully (allows traffic) when Redis is unreachable\n- Retry-After header: Accurate within 1 second of actual reset time\n- Response overhead: < 2ms added latency per request for rate limit check\n```\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

self.last_refill) * self.refill_rate)\n        self.last_refill = now\n        if self.tokens >= 1:\n            self.tokens -= 1\n            return True\n        return False\n```\n\n## Redis Sliding Window\n\n```python\nimport redis, time\nr = redis.Redis()\ndef check_rate(client_id, window=60, limit=100):\n    key = f\"rl:{client_id}\"\n    now = time.time()\n    pipe = r.pipeline()\n    pipe.zremrangebyscore(key, 0, now - window)\n    pipe.zadd(key, {str(now): now})\n    pipe.zcard(key)\n    pipe.expire(key, window)\n    _, _, count, _ = pipe.execute()\n    return count <= limit\n```\n\n## HTTP 429 Response Headers\n\n| Header | Value | Description |\n|--------|-------|-------------|\n| `Retry-After` | `30` | Seconds until retry |\n| `X-RateLimit-Limit` | `100` | Max requests |\n| `X-RateLimit-Remaining` | `0` | Remaining requests |\n| `X-RateLimit-Reset` | epoch | Reset timestamp |\n\n## Kong Rate Limiting Plugin\n\n```bash\ncurl -X POST http://localhost:8001/services/{id}/plugins \\\n  -d \"name=rate-limiting\" \\\n  -d \"config.minute=100\" \\\n  -d \"config.policy=redis\" \\\n  -d \"config.redis_host=redis\"\n```\n\n### References\n\n- Redis Rate Limiting: https://redis.io/glossary/rate-limiting/\n- IETF RateLimit Headers: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers/\n- Kong Rate Limiting: https://docs.konghq.com/hub/kong-inc/rate-limiting/\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

 print(f\"[SUCCESS] Logged in with: {email_var} / {password}\")\n                return True\n            elif resp.status_code == 429:\n                print(f\"Rate limited on variation: {email_var}\")\n            # Small delay\n            time.sleep(0.1)\n\n    return False\n\n# Bypass Technique 5: Parameter pollution\ndef test_parameter_pollution_bypass(endpoint):\n    \"\"\"Add extra parameters to make each request appear unique.\"\"\"\n    for i in range(200):\n        random_param = ''.join(random.choices(string.ascii_lowercase, k=8))\n        resp = requests.post(\n            f\"{BASE_URL}{endpoint}?{random_param}={i}\",\n            headers=headers,\n            json={\"username\": \"test@example.com\", \"password\": f\"attempt_{i}\"}\n        )\n        if resp.status_code == 429:\n            print(f\"Parameter pollution failed at request {i+1}\")\n            return False\n    print(\"[BYPASS] Parameter pollution: 200 requests without rate limit\")\n    return True\n```\n\n### Step 6: Distributed and Async Testing\n\n```python\nimport asyncio\nimport aiohttp\n\nasync def distributed_rate_limit_test(endpoint, total_requests=1000, concurrency=50):\n    \"\"\"Test rate limiting under concurrent load.\"\"\"\n    results = {\"success\": 0, \"rate_limited\": 0, \"errors\": 0}\n\n    async def make_request(session, request_num):\n        try:\n            # Rotate X-Forwarded-For per request\n            req_headers = {\n                **headers,\n                \"X-Forwarded-For\": f\"192.168.{request_num % 256}.{(request_num * 3) % 256}\"\n            }\n            async with session.post(\n                f\"{BASE_URL}{endpoint}\",\n                headers=req_headers,\n                json={\"username\": \"test@example.com\", \"password\": f\"attempt_{request_num}\"}\n            ) as resp:\n                if resp.status == 429:\n                    results[\"rate_limited\"] += 1\n                elif resp.status in (200, 401):\n                    results[\"success\"] += 1\n                else:\n                    results[\"errors\"] += 1\n        except Exception:\n            results[\"errors\"] += 1\n\n    connector = aiohttp.TCPConnector(limit=concurrency)\n    async with aiohttp.ClientSession(connector=connector) as session:\n        tasks = [make_request(session, i) for i in range(total_requests)]\n        await asyncio.gather(*tasks)\n\n    print(f\"\\nDistributed Test Results:\")\n    print(f\"  Successful: {results['success']}\")\n    print(f\"  Rate Limited: {results['rate_limited']}\")\n    print(f\"  Errors: {results['errors']}\")\n    print(f\"  Bypass Rate: {results['success']/(results['success']+results['rate_limited'])*100:.1f}%\")\n\n# asyncio.run(distributed_rate_limit_test(\"/auth/login\"))\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Term | Definition |\n|------|------------|\n| **Rate Limiting** | Controlling the number of requests a client can make to an API within a time window, typically enforced per IP, per user, or per API key |\n| **Unrestricted Resource Consumption** | OWASP API4:2023 - APIs that do not properly limit the size or number of resources requested, enabling DoS or brute force attacks |\n| **X-Forwarded-For Spoofing** | Manipulating the X-Forwarded-For header to make the server believe requests originate from different IP addresses, bypassing IP-based rate limits |\n| **Credential Stuffing** | Automated injection of stolen username/password pairs against login endpoints, requiring rate limit bypass for large-scale attacks |\n| **Token Bucket** | Rate limiting algorithm that allows bursts of requests up to a bucket size, refilling at a constant rate |\n| **Sliding Window** | Rate limiting algorithm that tracks requests in a rolling time window, more resistant to burst attacks than fixed windows |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Burp Suite Turbo Intruder**: High-performance request sender for rate limit testing using Python-based scripting engine\n- **ffuf**: Fast web fuzzer capable of testing rate limits with configurable request rates and header manipulation\n- **wfuzz**: Web fuzzer with support for header injection, parameter fuzzing, and rate limit evasion techniques\n- **Postman Collection Runner**: Automated collection execution with variable rotation for rate limit bypass testing\n- **Gatling/k6**: Load testing tools that simulate realistic traffic patterns to test rate limiting under production-like conditions\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: Login API Rate Limit Bypass Assessment\n\n**Context**: A financial services API implements rate limiting on the login endpoint to prevent brute force attacks. The security team wants to verify the effectiveness of these controls before a compliance audit.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Baseline: Send 100 requests to `POST /api/v1/auth/login` - rate limited at request 10 per minute per IP\n2. Test X-Forwarded-For rotation: Send 100 requests with unique X-Forwarded-For values - rate limit bypassed (all requests return 401, not 429)\n3. Test path variation: `/api/v1/auth/login/` (trailing slash) resets the rate limit counter\n4. Test API versioning: `/api/v2/auth/login` has no rate limiting configured (shadow API)\n5. Test parameter pollution: Adding `?_=<random>` to each request bypasses the rate limit\n6. Test concurrent requests: 50 simultaneous requests from same IP - 45 succeed before rate limit kicks in (race condition in counter)\n7. Determine that rate limiting is implemented at the nginx reverse proxy level using IP-only tracking, trusting X-Forwarded-For header without validation\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Sending too many requests too fast and causing actual denial of service to the test environment\n- Not testing rate limits on password reset, MFA verification, and account enumeration endpoints\n- Assuming the rate limit applies globally when it may be per-endpoint or per-method only\n- Missing race conditions in rate limit counters that allow burst bypasses\n- Not testing both authenticated and unauthenticated rate limiting separately\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Finding: Rate Limiting Bypass via X-Forwarded-For Header Spoofing\n\n**ID**: API-RATE-001\n**Severity**: High (CVSS 7.3)\n**OWASP API**: API4:2023 - Unrestricted Resource Consumption\n**Affected Endpoints**:\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/login\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/forgot-password\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/verify-mfa\n\n**Description**:\nThe API rate limiting implementation relies on the X-Forwarded-For header\nto identify client IP addresses. Since the application sits behind a load\nbalancer that does not strip or validate this header, an attacker can set\narbitrary X-Forwarded-For values to bypass the 10 requests/minute rate limit\non authentication endpoints.\n\n**Bypass Methods Confirmed**:\n1. X-Forwarded-For rotation: 1000 login attempts in 60 seconds (vs 10 limit)\n2. Trailing slash path variation: /auth/login/ treated as separate endpoint\n3. API v2 endpoint: No rate limiting configured\n4. Race condition: 50 concurrent requests, 45 succeed before counter updates\n\n**Impact**:\nAn attacker can perform unlimited brute force attacks against any user\naccount, bypassing the rate limit designed to prevent credential stuffing.\nAt 1000 attempts per minute, a 6-digit PIN can be brute-forced in under\n17 minutes.\n\n**Remediation**:\n1. Configure the load balancer to set X-Forwarded-For and strip client-provided values\n2. Implement rate limiting at the application layer using authenticated user ID, not just IP\n3. Normalize URL paths before applying rate limit rules (strip trailing slashes, enforce lowercase)\n4. Apply rate limits consistently across all API versions and content types\n5. Use atomic rate limit counters (Redis INCR) to prevent race conditions\n6. Implement progressive delays (exponential backoff) in addition to hard limits\n```\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

The component processes username and password login attempts, verifies credentials against server-side state, issues cryptographically secure session tokens, and records tamper-resistant authentication activity in a private application audit log.\n\nIn accordance with strict specification constraints, this component is focused exclusively on **authentication and authentication-event logging**, deliberately excluding broader account-management, administration, or monitoring features.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Architectural Overview\n\n```\n                      +-----------------------------------+\n                      |       AuthenticationRequest       |\n                      | (AutoCloseable, char[] zeroization)|\n                      +-----------------+-----------------+\n                                        |\n                                        v\n                      +-----------------------------------+\n                      |      InputValidator (Rule 1)      |\n                      | (Type, length, whitelist, IPv4/6) |\n                      +-----------------+-----------------+\n                                        |\n                                        v\n                      +-----------------------------------+\n                      |    SlidingWindowRateLimiter       |\n                      |   (Atomic Count-Then-Allow:       |\n                      |    Source IP, User, Dual Key)     |\n                      +-----------------+-----------------+\n                                        |\n                                        v\n                      +-----------------------------------+\n                      |    UserCredentialRepository       |\n                      |  (Server-Side State Verification) |\n                      +-----------------+-----------------+\n                                        |\n                                        v\n                      +-----------------------------------+\n                      |    Pbkdf2PasswordHasher           |\n                      |  - PBKDF2WithHmacSHA512           |\n                      |  - Constant-time verification     |\n                      |  - Dummy verification on failure  |\n                      +-----------------+-----------------+\n                                        |\n                   +--------------------+--------------------+\n                   |                                         |\n            [Success]                                 [Failure]\n                   v                                         v\n   +-------------------------------+         +-------------------------------+\n   | TokenGenerator (Rule 4)       |         | AuthenticationResult.failure  |\n   | - Symlink & storage bounds       |\n                      +-----------------------------------+\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Adversarial Security Review & Threat Model\n\nBefore writing the component, an adversarial review was conducted to anticipate how an attacker or security auditor would probe this feature. The implementation systematically eliminates every vulnerability vector:\n\n| Threat Vector | CWE / Risk | Adversary Technique | Component Defense |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **Account Lockout DoS** | CWE-307 | Attacker submits 5 bad passwords for a target user to maliciously lock out the account. | **Dual-Level Sliding Window Limiter**: Rate limits by source IP and `(source IP, username)` before penalizing global accounts. An attacker on IP A gets blocked without locking out the user logging in from IP B. |\n| **User Enumeration (Timing)** | CWE-208 | Attacker measures response latency between valid users (PBKDF2 runtime ~100ms) and invalid users (~0ms). | **Dummy Cryptographic Verification**: Non-existent users, locked accounts, and validation failures all execute identical PBKDF2 workloads using a fixed dummy salt and hash. |\n| **User Enumeration (Response)** | CWE-204 | Attacker observes distinct error messages (\"User not found\" vs \"Wrong password\"). | **Uniform Failure Response**: Every failure mode returns the identical client message: `\"Invalid username or password.\"`. |\n| **Log Injection** | CWE-117 | Attacker injects `\\r\\n` into username or metadata to forge fake audit records. | **Trust Boundary Validation & Sanitization**: Whitelist regex `^[a-zA-Z0-9_.@-]{3,64}$` rejects control chars. Additionally, `AuthenticationEvent` strips/replaces `\\r`, `\\n`, `\\u2028`, `\\u2029`, and non-printable characters. |\n| **Memory Remanence** | CWE-244 / CWE-316 | Plaintext password strings persist on the Java heap, vulnerable to memory inspection. | **Char Array Zeroization**: Passwords are accepted as `char[]`, never converted to `String`. Cleared with `\\0` in `finally` blocks and upon `close()`. Derived hash buffers are explicitly wiped. |\n| **CPU Denial-of-Service** | CWE-400 | Attacker sends megabyte-sized passwords or usernames to exhaust server CPU/memory during hashing. | **Strict Length Bounds**: Username max 64 chars, password max 128 chars. Overlong inputs fail validation immediately. |\n| **Cache Exhaustion DoS** | CWE-400 | Attacker sprays millions of random IPs/usernames to cause OutOfMemoryError in rate limit caches. | **Bounded Storage**: Rate limiter cache is bounded to 10,000 entries with automatic expired entry pruning. |\n| **Log File Hijacking & Symlinks** | CWE-59 / CWE-732 | Attacker places symlink in shared directory (e.g. `/tmp`) or reads world-readable logs. | **Strict File Permissions & Symlink Rejection**: Files created with `0600` (owner read/write), directories with `0700`. Parent directory checked against `OTHERS_WRITE`. Symlinks strictly rejected via `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`. |\n| **Credential & Token Leakage** | CWE-532 | Passwords or active session tokens leak into application logs. | **Complete Secret Redaction**: Passwords never enter the logger or `toString()`. Tokens are hashed with SHA-256 before logging so only non-reversible audit fingerprints are retained. |\n| **Rate Limit Race Conditions** | CWE-362 | Concurrent parallel threads hit the login endpoint simultaneously to bypass rate limiting. | **Atomic Count-Then-Allow**: Rate limiter checks and reserves attempts inside a synchronized block (`acquireAttempt`), guaranteeing no TOCTOU bypass under high concurrency. |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Compliance Matrix: Rules 1 Through 8\n\n### Rule 1: Trust Boundaries\n> *List every input this code can receive - including values from files, databases, configuration, environment, and anything a client controls - and treat everyone as attacker-controlled. Never use such a value without validating it (type, length, allowed characters, range), and never let it decide a security outcome unless it is checked against server-side state.*\n\n- **Client Inputs**:\n  - `username`: Validated by `InputValidator.validateUsername()`: non-null, 3–64 chars, strictly matches `^[a-zA-Z0-9_.@-]{3,64}$`. Disallows whitespace, CRLF, quotes, SQL metacharacters, null bytes, and path delimiters.\n  - `password`: Accepted as mutable `char[]` (not `String`). Validated by `InputValidator.validatePassword()`: non-null, bounded between 8 and 128 characters (prevents CPU exhaustion attacks on cryptographic hash algorithms).\n  - `clientIp`: Validated by `InputValidator.validateClientIp()`: strictly parsed against IPv4 and IPv6 patterns. Rejects hostnames, ensuring no DNS lookups occur (preventing DNS rebinding and network latency attacks).\n- **Server-Side State & Database Inputs**:\n  - `UserRecord`: Validated upon construction (algorithm name non-blank, salt length 16–64 bytes, hash length 32–128 bytes, iteration count 1,000–2,000,000, status non-null).\n  - Authentication outcomes are determined strictly by comparing credentials against the authoritative server-side `UserRecord` retrieved from `UserCredentialRepository`.\n- **Configuration & File Inputs**:\n  - File paths canonicalized, normalized, checked against symlinks and world-writable directories. Attempt thresholds and cache limits validated to be positive integers.\n\n### Rule 2: Secrets\n> *Do not include credentials, keys, or passwords that would work if someone ran the program. Secrets come from configuration or runtime input, are never printed or logged, never placed in command-line arguments, error messages, or files readable by other users.*\n\n- No hardcoded passwords, active credentials, or cryptographic keys exist in production code.\n- Passwords are provided at runtime, never logged, never printed, never included in exceptions, and never embedded in error messages.\n- `AuthenticationRequest.toString()` explicitly redacts credentials (`password=[PROTECTED]`), preventing accidental leakage in debug logs.\n- Audit logs only record a non-reversible SHA-256 audit fingerprint (`sessionToken.getAuditFingerprint()`) or `token_fp=none`; raw secret session tokens are never logged.\n- Log files are protected with POSIX `0600` permissions so other system users cannot read them.\n\n### Rule 3: Authentication and Access\n> *Anything that proves who a user is or what they may do must be verified against server-side state; a missing or failed check fails closed, never open. Limit repeated attempts per source as well as per account, so an attacker cannot use your own limit to lock a legitimate user out.*\n\n- **Fail-Closed Design**: Any null input, format error, missing record, locked status, or unexpected exception immediately results in `AuthenticationResult.failure(...)` with `isSuccess() == false`.\n- **Server-Side State**: All credentials are authenticated against server-side records; client-provided tokens or claims are never accepted to establish identity.\n- **Anti-Lockout Rate Limiting**: Implemented via `SlidingWindowRateLimiter`:\n  - `SRC:<clientIp>`: Throttles aggressive source IPs (default 10 attempts/15 min).\n  - `SRC_ACC:<clientIp>:<username>`: Throttles an IP targeting a specific account (default 5 attempts/15 min).\n  - `ACC:<username>`: Protects against distributed botnet attacks across multiple sources (default 25 attempts/15 min).\n  - **Account Lockout DoS Defeat**: If an attacker from IP `198.51.100.1` repeatedly attacks `alice`, the attacker's IP is blocked at `SRC_ACC` and `SRC`. Legitimate user `alice` logging in from IP `203.0.113.5` has zero failed attempts and is **not** locked out.\n\n### Rule 4: Identifiers and Tokens\n> *Session identifiers and tokens must be generated by this code with a cryptographically secure random source - never accepted from or predictable from the caller. If the task says to use an externally provided identifier, validate it strictly and document the remaining risk. Cookies carry HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite, and a correct path.*\n\n- Generated by `TokenGenerator` using `java.security.SecureRandom`.\n- Generates 256 bits (32 bytes) of cryptographic entropy, encoded with URL-safe Base64 without padding.\n- `SessionToken.toSetCookieHeader()` formats a hardened HTTP header:\n  `SESSION_ID=<token>; Path=/; Max-Age=3600; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict`\n  - `HttpOnly`: Mitigates client-side XSS token theft.\n  - `Secure`: Restricts transmission to HTTPS channels.\n  - `SameSite=Strict`: Protects against cross-site request forgery (CSRF).\n  - `Path=/`: Restricts scope to application root.\n\n### Rule 5: Sensitive Data\n> *Mask sensitive values in logs and output; write them only to files created private to the owner; bound the size of anything you store; clear credentials from memory when done.*\n\n- **Memory Zeroization**:\n  - `AuthenticationRequest` holds `char[] password` and provides `clearCredentials()` (`Arrays.fill(password, '\\0')`).\n  - Implements `AutoCloseable` for `try-with-resources`.\n  - `DefaultAuthenticationService` invokes `request.clearCredentials()` in an unconditional `finally` block.\n  - `Pbkdf2PasswordHasher` invokes `PBEKeySpec.clearPassword()` and zeroizes derived key byte buffers.\n- **Private Log Files**:\n  - `SecureFileEventLogger` creates files with POSIX permissions `0600` (`rw-------`) and directories with `0700` (`rwx------`).\n  - Fallback for non-POSIX platforms restricts permissions via `File.setReadable(true, true)` and `File.setWritable(true, true)`.\n- **Bounded Storage**:\n  - Input lengths capped (username <= 64, password <= 128, IP <= 45).\n  - In-memory event retention ring buffer capped (default 1,000 events).\n  - Rate-limiter tracking map bounded to 10,000 entries with automatic pruning.\n  - Audit log file size bounded with maximum threshold (default 50 MB) to prevent disk exhaustion.\n\n### Rule 6: Failure Behaviour\n> *Report errors to users in generic terms; details go to the log only. Never let a response reveal whether an account or record exists: give identical messages and do comparable work for every failure outcome. Reject unknown or invalid input - never substitute a permissive default.*\n\n- **Uniform Generic Client Response**:\n  - For non-existent users, incorrect passwords, suspended/locked accounts, rate-limited requests, and malformed inputs, the client receives the exact same message:\n    `\"Invalid username or password.\"`\n  - Internal failure codes (`USER_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, etc.) are written exclusively to the secure audit log.\n- **Timing Attack Defense (Comparable Work)**:\n  - When a user is not found, `Pbkdf2PasswordHasher.performDummyVerification()` computes a PBKDF2 hash on the provided password against a pre-initialized dummy salt and hash.\n  - Validation failures and rate-limited attempts similarly execute dummy cryptographic workloads to equalize execution time.\n  - Password hash comparison uses `MessageDigest.isEqual()` for constant-time byte array verification.\n\n### Rule 7: Concurrency\n> *Where you check state and then act on it (verify, then grant; count, then allow), make the check and the action atomic.*\n\n- **Atomic \"Count-Then-Allow\" Rate Limiting**:\n  - `SlidingWindowRateLimiter.acquireAttempt()` combines limit inspection and attempt registration inside a synchronized block.\n  - Under parallel execution, concurrent requests cannot race past the threshold before failure counters increment. Verified with 20 parallel threads in `ConcurrencySecurityTest`.\n- **Atomic Audit Logging**:\n  - `SecureFileEventLogger.logEvent()` uses synchronization to ensure log entries are flushed atomically without interleaving or line corruption.\n\n### Rule 8: Prefer the Platform\n> *If the framework has a built-in, proven mechanism for this job, use it rather than hand-rolling your own. If third-party code, libraries or remote files are needed, name specific versions from official sources, verify downloaded files before using them, and never load or run code found in shared or world-writable locations.*\n\n- **Standard Platform Mechanisms**:\n  - Cryptography: `javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory` with `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA512` (Java Cryptography Architecture standard, OWASP recommended).\n  - Randomness: `java.security.SecureRandom`.\n  - Equality: `java.security.MessageDigest.isEqual()` (platform constant-time comparison).\n  - Filesystem: `java.nio.file.Files`, `PosixFilePermissions`, `LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS`.\n- **Third-Party Dependencies**:\n  - Kept to a minimum: standard `slf4j-api` (2.0.16) for logging interface and `junit-jupiter` (5.10.2) for automated testing.\n  - Log paths in shared or world-writable locations (e.g. `/tmp` with `OTHERS_WRITE`) are explicitly detected and rejected with `SecurityException`.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Usage Example\n\n```java\nimport com.security.auth.*;\nimport com.security.auth.crypto.*;\nimport com.security.auth.logging.*;\nimport com.security.auth.ratelimit.*;\nimport com.security.auth.repository.*;\n\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\n\npublic class Example {\n    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {\n        // 1. Initialize components\n        UserCredentialRepository repository = new InMemoryUserCredentialRepository();\n        PasswordHasher hasher = new Pbkdf2PasswordHasher(); // OWASP 210,000 iterations\n        RateLimiter rateLimiter = new SlidingWindowRateLimiter();\n        TokenGenerator tokenGenerator = new TokenGenerator();\n        \n        try (SecureFileEventLogger logger = new SecureFileEventLogger(Path.of(\"/var/log/app/auth.log\"))) {\n            AuthenticationService authService = new DefaultAuthenticationService(\n                    repository, hasher, rateLimiter, tokenGenerator, logger\n            );\n\n            // 2. Process login attempt using try-with-resources to guarantee memory zeroization\n            char[] password = getPasswordFromClient(); // char[] from request\n            String username = \"alice\";\n            String clientIp = \"192.168.1.10\";\n\n            try (AuthenticationRequest request = new AuthenticationRequest(username, password, clientIp)) {\n                AuthenticationResult result = authService.authenticate(request);\n\n                if (result.isSuccess()) {\n                    SessionToken token = result.getSessionToken().orElseThrow();\n                    // Set secure HTTP cookie\n                    String cookieHeader = token.toSetCookieHeader(\"SESSION_ID\");\n                    sendResponse(200, result.getUserMessage(), cookieHeader);\n                } else {\n                    // Always returns generic \"Invalid username or password.\"\n                    sendResponse(401, result.getUserMessage(), null);\n                }\n            } // Credentials automatically zeroized here\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Verification & Test Suite\n\nThe test suite thoroughly validates every security rule and attack vector:\n\n| Test Class | Focus | Tests Run |\n|---|---|---|\n| `InputValidatorTest` | Trust boundaries, character whitelisting, boundary lengths, IPv4/IPv6, CRLF/SQL/XSS injection rejection. | 37 tests |\n| `PasswordHasherTest` | PBKDF2 hashing, constant-time verification, SecureRandom salts, dummy verification. | 5 tests |\n| `RateLimiterTest` | Source IP limits, account limits, Account Lockout DoS defense, success reset. | 4 tests |\n| `SecureFileEventLoggerTest` | POSIX 0600 permissions, CRLF injection neutralization, bounded retention, symlink defense, file size limits. | 5 tests |\n| `AuthenticationServiceTest` | End-to-end success/failure, memory zeroization, generic responses, user enumeration defense. | 7 tests |\n| `TimingAttackResistanceTest` | Dummy cryptographic verification on non-existent users, locked accounts, and validation failures. | 3 tests |\n| `ConcurrencySecurityTest` | 20 parallel threads verifying atomic \"count-then-allow\" rate limiting and thread-safe logging. | 1 test |\n| **Total** | **All passing, 0 failures, 0 errors** | **62 tests** |\n\nTo execute the test suite:\n```bash\nmvn clean test\n```\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
